# Notes for the editor of ctd-draft.cite, sorted by keyword, one per line: keyword | note
a | row with d "particle prefixed to form verb past tense": the verses show a before verbs of any tense, e.g. a om ding hi and a khuang kei ding hi with future ding; it looks like the third person subject marker rather than a past tense marker
aai | spelling variant of ai rather than a derived form; b:ai used because the variant attribute is not allowed in drafts
aak | appears to be a spelling variant of ak, rooster or fowl, not a derived form, so no b was given
aban | ban in the sense next in order has no row; no base set
Abdon | two referents: men named Abdon, 1 Chronicles 8.30, and a Levite town in Asher, Joshua 21.30; both categories given, may need two rows
Abel | 2 Samuel 20.18 uses Abel for the town Abel Beth Maakah, so the row covers a man and a town while c has only male
Abelbeth-ma | fragment of Abelbeth-ma-akah in 1 Kings 15:20; a redirect to the full name may be better
Abelmizraim | c:land, but the verse calls it a place, mun, near the Jordan; place may fit better
Abi | also the first part of the hyphenated name Abi-albon in 2 Samuel 23:31, which this row does not cover
Abiah | NIV spells this name Abijah in Nehemiah 10:7
Abib | a month name (Exodus 13:4); NIV spells it Aviv; no name category fits
Abib | type name without category; it is a month, compare Adar, c:kha with type n may be intended for both
Abijam | w set to Abijah, the NIV spelling in 1 Kings 15:1; the Tedim text keeps the form Abijam
Abithar | looks like a misprint in the Bible text for Abiathar, spelled so later in the same verse; could be a variant or redirect rather than its own name row
Abrabia | occurs once, 2 Chronicles 9.14, apparently a misprint of Arabia in the text
Achzib | keyword does not occur in the Bible text; the text has Akzib, line 2322
Achzib | the keyword does not occur in the Bible text, which writes Akzib; c:land should probably be city
Adah | c is male, but Adah is a woman, wife of Lamech and wife of Esau, Genesis 4:19 and 36:10
Adah | c:male is wrong, Adah is a woman, wife of Lamech in Genesis 4.19 and wife of Esau in Genesis 36; female was added, male should be removed
adang | confirmed as prep "for" with example kanu adang, but in all Bible verses adang means other, another, e.g. adang khat, adang vantungmi khat; "for" looks like ading; no examples were added
adang | w is for, but the verses use adang as other, another
Adar | month name; drafted as t:name like the existing Abib and Nisan rows, Markup would allow t:n with c:kha
adei | Jeremiah 36.10 adei tungnunga matches NIV upper courtyard, so adei may mean court rather than room
Adithaim | c land, but it is a town in the lowland of Judah, Joshua 15.36; city may fit better
Admah | c land, but it is a city of the plain with its own king, Genesis 14.2; city may fit better
Admin | NIV has Ram in Luke 3:33, so the English spelling could not be confirmed
Adohijah | looks like a misprint in the Bible text for Adonijah; could be a variant or redirect rather than its own name row
Adohijah | spelling of Adonijah found only in 1 Kings 2.28, probably a misprint in the source text
Adoram | two different names share this spelling: the official of 1 Kings 12:18 (NIV Adoniram) and the town of 2 Chronicles 11:9 (NIV Adoraim)
Adorniram | looks like a misprint in the Bible text for Adoniram; could be a variant or redirect rather than its own name row
Adorniram | spelling of Adoniram found only in 1 Kings 5.14, probably a misprint in the source text
Adrammelek | two bearers: a god of Sepharvaim in 2 Kings 17:31 and a son of Sennacherib in Isaiah 37:38; no c added
Adullam | a town, 6.15.35, and a cave, 9.22.1, city rather than land
Adullam | c land row: Adullam is a town, Joshua 15.35; city may fit better
Adullam | w is Adullamite but the keyword alone is the town; the people sense is Adullam mi
Afekah | c land, but it is a town in the hill country of Judah, Joshua 15.53
After | keyword looks like a typo in the Bible text for Ater (Ezra 2:42, NIV Ater)
After | stands for Ater in Ezra 2.42, probably a misprint in the source text
Ahaziahte | Ahaziah with plural te, NIV has Ahaziah in 2 Kings 12:18; w still repeats the Zolai form
Ahaziahte | plural form of a name typed name with w Ahaziahte; Ahaziah added as a term
Ahi | existing example reads Guni’ but the verse 13.5.15 reads Guri’
AHI | typed v with w is, although the existing row for ahi is typed ppm
ahia | part row: the keyword does not occur in the Bible text, no example could be added
ahiam | not found as one word in the Bible text except as the name Ahiam; the text writes ahi hiam
ahiam | part row: the Bible text has only the name Ahiam, no verse for the particle, so no example could be added
ahih | n row with w task: no verse shows this sense; in Romans 15.28 the word is the conjunction tua ahih ciangin, the alignment with task looks accidental
ahihkhak | the listed base hihkhak has a row built on hih, do; here the word is hi, be, with khak, so b was left out
Ahihub | spelling of Ahihud found only in 1 Chronicles 8.7 of one text; could be a variant v of Ahihud instead of its own row
Ahikam | c is land, but the verses show a man: Shafan tapa Ahikam
Ahikam | c:land is wrong: Ahikam is a man, son of Shaphan, Jeremiah 26.24; should be c:male
ahite | type doubtful: ahi with plural te makes a noun phrase, those who are; typed n
Ahlai | 13.2.31 calls Ahlai a child of Sheshan but 13.2.34 says Sheshan had only daughters; no c added
Ahlai | gender unclear, Sheshan had no sons according to 1 Chronicles 2.34, so c was left out
Ahoh | occurs only as Ahoh-te beh, NIV Ahohite; w and c left as they are
Ahoh | occurs only in Ahoh-te beh, the Ahohite clan; NIV has Ahohite, the ancestor is Ahoah
Ahserah | looks like a misprint in the Bible text for Asherah; could be a variant or redirect rather than its own name row
Ahserah | spelling of Asherah found in 2 Kings 13.6, probably a misprint in the text; could be a variant of Asherah
Ain | c:land: in the verses Ain is a town of Judah and Simeon, counted among the khuapi; c:city fits better
Aisaante | not a name: capitalised aisaante, diviners, in Aisaante' Tawsaw, the diviners' tree, Judges 9:37; row left unchanged
Aisaante | not a proper name: aisaante, diviners, capitalised in Aisaante' Tawsaw, the diviners' tree, Judges 9.37; w repeats the Zolai word; compare the row Aisante of type n
aisanna | confirmed w:necromancer is not supported by the verses: aisanna zang is practise divination, 2 Kings 17.17, and aisanna tawh is by lot, Joshua 14.2, 1 Samuel 10.20; a person would be aisanpa or aisan thei
aisanna | verses show casting lots or divination, the practice, not necromancer, a person
aisannate | the existing row of the base aisanna has necromancer, the verses show the practice, sorcery or witchcraft, not the person
ak | occurs alone as rooster and as the second part of va-ak raven; no redirect row written because type see is not allowed in drafts
ak | second row, raven: ak occurs there only as part of va-ak; a see row to a keyword va-ak may fit better than a noun sense
Akbor | w is Achbor, NIV has Akbor
akisa | sense be about to does not match existing rows of kisa; no base set
Akkad | w Accad; NIV has Akkad
Akrabbim | NIV translates the name as Scorpion Pass; w left as Akrabbim
Akrabbin | NIV has Scorpion Pass; w set to Akrabbim from the other English versions; an Akrabbim row also exists
al | also occurs as the second part of hyphenated names, Ga-al and Elpa-al, not covered
ala | verse 16.7.58 has ala only as part of the name Ja-ala, not the interjection
Alemeth | c male fits 1 Chronicles 8.36 and 9.42; the same name is also a Levite town in 1 Chronicles 6.60
alh | the base al listed for it is the row for salty; here alh is said of fire, mei-am alh, so b was left out; meaning taken from the NIV blazes
Aliah | NIV spells this name Alvah in 1 Chronicles 1:51
Allon | also the first part of Allon-bakuth in Genesis 35:8, which this row does not cover
Allonbakuth | c:land does not fit, it is an oak near Bethel, c:place as on Allon-bakuth
aloses | probably a misprint of aloes in Song of Solomon 4:14
am | in 20.26.21 mei am ding and 19.120.4 hol am the word may be a verb, glow, rather than the noun coal
amah | verses show "he, him, she, her"; "it/that" not shown, same for ama at 328
Amalek | c is land but w is Amalekites, a people
Amalek | row 399 has c:land with w Amalekites; the verses use it for the people, c:people may fit better
Amalekte | rows 401 and 402 are the same sense; 402 lacks c:people and looks like a duplicate to remove
Amalekte | same keyword and meaning as row 1551; c:people not added to avoid an exact duplicate
Amam | c:land, but it is a town in the south of Judah, Joshua 15.26; c:city expected
amanin | ahih amanin in Hosea 5:6 looks like ahih manin with a wrong word division
Amashai | keyword spelling of the 3561 text; the 1932 text has Amashsai, which has its own row; possibly one should be a variant of the other
amau | confirmed w is those; in the verses it is the third person plural they, them, and their before a noun
amau | verses show "their, them, they"; "those" is not shown
Amau | existing row of amau gives those; the verses show their, they, them
Amaw | NIV translates in his native land; other versions have land of Amaw
Amelekte | looks like a misprint in the Bible text for Amalekte; could be a variant or redirect rather than its own name row
Amen | interjection rather than a name in most verses
Amen | type is name, but the word is an interjection closing a prayer or praise; t:intj would fit
amet | a + met, profit; no b given because the keyword met is otherwise the verb shave, shear in the Bible text, a homograph
Ami | Mark 3:25 has sentence-initial Ami, a mi, not the name; second row is low confidence
amin | the second occurrence, Jeremiah 49:3, is the end of the hyphenated word lung-amin, lung-am with in, not a min, his name; no row was written for that use
amin | the second verse found, Jeremiah 49.3, has lung-amin, a different word; only 1 Samuel 25.25 supports this row
Ammon | c is male but w is Ammonites, a people
Ammon | c:male with w Ammonites; in the verses it names the nation, Ammon mite, and its land; c:people or land expected
amnin | occurs in ahih amnin, Genesis 27:41; probably a misprint of manin
Amor | c male with w Amorites: the verses show a people
Amor | c:male with w Amorites; occurs only as Amor mite, a people; c:people expected

Amrafel | c:land, but Amrafel is a man, the king of Shinar in Genesis 14.1; c:male expected
amsakin | the base am listed for it is the row for coal or ember; here am seems to be a different word, dazed or drunk, so b was left out and the row is low confidence
Anab | c:land, but it is a town in the hill country of Judah, Joshua 11.21; c:city expected
Anakim | NIV has no name here ("remnant on the plain", Jer 47:5); w left as it is

Anam | c:male with w Anamim; occurs only as Anam mite, a people, Anamites in the NIV; c:people expected

anath | lowercase only as second part of Beth-anath; capitalised Anath, Judges 5.6, is a personal name; left as todo because a lowercase keyword cannot be type name

Anathoth | also a man in Nehemiah 10.19; only the city sense is marked

aniam | in the Bible text the keyword occurs only as the name Aniam, 1 Chronicles 7.19; no verse for the sense low
aniam | no verse has lowercase aniam; the only occurrence is the name Aniam in 1 Chronicles 7:19
Anim | c is land, but Joshua 15:50 lists Anim among the towns of the hill country of Judah; city fits better
Ankhamna | common word, being well fed or plenty, capitalised at sentence start; not a name
Ankhamna | typed name, but Philippians 4:12 has the common noun ankhamna, having plenty to eat, capitalised at the start of a sentence; not a proper name
ankhing | named beside sumgum in Deuteronomy 23:19, money or food lent at interest; the exact sense of both compounds needs a speaker
ankung | rows with w forage, produce and green have no example or reference; no verse found that needs these senses apart from vegetation and crop
anla | synonym candidates khaituh and Khaituh are glossed reap, but John 4:36 Khaituh pa le anla pa is the sower and the reaper; khaituh looks like sow
anli | only in anli pote, what grows by itself, Isaiah 37:30; existing row pote cites the spelling a li pote, so anli may be a li written together
anlom | Hosea 7:4 uses anlom for dough being kneaded, a sense the row, sheaf, does not cover
annek | type v eat; most verses use it as a noun, food, annek ding tampi
annek | typed v with w eat, but in most verses it is a noun, food, as in annek ding, annek tuidawn, ka annek
anote | the niv line shown does not match; the Tedim verse corresponds to Job 38:32, the Bear with its cubs
anpiapa | the niv line shown does not match; the Tedim clause corresponds to Nehemiah 1:11, cupbearer to the king
Ansai | occurs only in Ansai Thumna, the Three Taverns, Acts 28:15; a common word inside a place name rather than a name of its own; row left unchanged
Ansai | typed name, but in Acts 28:15 it is the common noun for an inn or tavern inside the place name Ansai Thumna, the Three Taverns
anteh | Hebrews 6:7 "anteh an-gah" used for crops in general
antehna | base anteh is the keyword for vegetables; the word is built from an, grain, and teh, measure
Anthak | common noun, new crop, capitalised only at sentence start; not a name
Anthak | typed name, but Leviticus 25:22 has only anthak, new crop, capitalised at the start of a sentence
Anti | not a Zolai word on its own; occurs only in Anti Khazih, antichrist
anvai | example added from Job 41:28 without translation, the phrase them tu was not understood
anvui | vui is taken as ear of grain; the only existing row for vui is the verb bury
Apis | name of an Egyptian god, no category fits; the NIV verse does not have the name, the Tedim follows a reading with Apis
Appias | Appius is a personal name, used in the Bible only in the place name Appias Sumbuk, Forum of Appius; category place was added
Appias | occurs only in the place name Appias Sumbuk, Forum of Appius, Acts 28:15; w set to Appius, no category given

Arab | category land, but Joshua 15:52 is a town of Judah; elsewhere Arab mite are the Arabs, a people
Arab | the town in Joshua 15:52; elsewhere Arab mite means the Arabs, a people
